{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — mvp_pipeline_pythia_002（进行中检查）\n\n**结论**: PASS（初步，pipeline 仍在运行）\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: info] **depth-0 LoRA 训练完成**：final loss 2.2411，3 epochs，268.3s，训练曲线平稳（loss 2.17-2.25 区间，grad_norm ~0.22）。LoRA adapter 已保存到 checkpoints/lora_depth_0。\n\n- [severity: info] **depth-1 生成进行中**：当前 ~605/5000 样本。模型加载和生成正常。\n\n- [severity: info] **域一致性初步通过（Pythia 选择验证成功）**：抽查 depth-1 head（seed_id 0-2）和 middle（seed_id 299-300）共 5 条样本，全部为自然语言文本：\n  - seed_id 0: 论坛帖子（VX4 引擎项目进展）\n  - seed_id 1: 学术文本（哺乳动物精子进化）\n  - seed_id 2: 商业产品描述（toddler beds）\n  - seed_id 299: Stack Overflow 风格 Q&A（树节点操作，自然语言叙述非原始代码）\n  - seed_id 300: 博客/随笔（关于夏天）\n  **零代码/HTML 污染**，与之前 Qwen2.5 的 100%（Instruct）/ 17.9%（Base）代码生成形成鲜明对比。\n\n- [severity: info] **extract_features.py 已适配 Pythia**：`--model_path` 默认值为 `/root/autodl-tmp/models/pythia-1.4b`，`--gpu` 默认 1（可与 pipeline 的 GPU 0 并行）。\n\n- [severity: info] **GPU 边界合规**：pipeline 在 cuda:0（13.7GB / 97.8GB，62% compute），cuda:1 空闲。两张卡均在项目分配范围内（ROADMAP: cuda:0, cuda:1）。\n\n- [severity: info] **Registry spec 完整**：mvp_pipeline_pythia_002 包含训练命令、服务器、GPU、数据路径+条数(5000)、模型名(EleutherAI/pythia-1.4b)、seed(42)、hypothesis、完整 hyperparams。符合追溯审查要求。\n\n- [severity: info] **seed data tokenizer 不一致（不阻塞）**：prepare_seed_data.py 使用 `Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B` tokenizer 筛选 256-512 tokens 的 Wikipedia 文本，但实际训练使用 Pythia tokenizer。由于两者都是 BPE 且筛选仅用于长度过滤（非精确语义操作），且 training max_seq_len=512 会截断过长样本，实际影响可忽略。论文写作时应注明 seed data 的 token 长度范围是近似值。\n\n### 待完成\n- depth-1 生成完成后做 tail 域一致性检查（当前只检查了 head+middle）\n- depth-2/3 的域一致性检查\n- 特征提取结果审查\n- MVP gate check 审查（4-class acc >40%, adjacent AUC >0.6）",

   - depth-2 generation: IN PROGRESS (depth_2.jsonl = 3.8MB, growing)
   
I'm also seeing that a parallel worker on GPU 1 completed feature extraction on depth_0 while the main pipeline ran on GPU 0 — that's smart parallelization. GPU 0 is currently at 76% compute handling depth-2 generation, while GPU 1 is now idle after finishing its feature extraction task. The loss improvement from depth-0 to depth-1 is substantial, dropping from 2.24 to 1.39. This makes sense — depth-1 text is model-generated so it's "easier" for the model to fit. This loss difference itself could be a useful signal for depth estimation!

5. **The worker**: `worker_infra_cuda1-parallel-tasks` seems to have run feature extraction on depth_0 using GPU 1. Let me check the data files more carefully.

Let me verify:
- depth_0_features.jsonl content
- depth_1.jsonl sample count (should be 5000)
- depth_2.jsonl sample count (still growing)
- Whether there's any GPU boundary issue with the parallel worker

The worker name suggests it was using cuda:1, which is within project allocation. But I need to verify.

The 38% loss drop from depth-0 to depth-1 is striking — if the model keeps improving at predicting its own outputs, that's strong evidence that recursive generation actually produces learnable patterns, which aligns with what I'm trying to show.
```

   - Lexical diversity metrics (TTR, hapax_ratio) and repetition patterns (2-gram and 3-gram) all fall within expected ranges, and the percentile-based PPL distribution captures the shape well

5. **Naming concern**: The features file is `depth_0_features.jsonl`, suggesting per-depth organization

, but the pipeline's extract_features.py defaults to outputting a single `data/features.jsonl`. The parallel worker likely used a custom output path, which could create confusion if the final extraction also tries to write to `features.jsonl`. I'm realizing the worker probably extracted features for depth_0 separately while the other depths are still being generated — a smart optimization to avoid waiting for everything to finish.

Looking at the feature records, each one includes a `"depth"` field, so these per-depth files can be merged together later without issues. Now I'm checking the training loss progression across depths: depth-0 shows a finetune loss of 2.2411 while depth-1 dropped to 1.3894, a roughly 38% improvement. This aligns with what model collapse research shows — the model progressively fits its own outputs better because the distribution becomes narrower and simpler than the original human text, which itself supports the collapse hypothesis. Let me examine what's happening at depth-2.

The problem of abnormal cells can be avoided by adding certain substances to the medium. The use of serum-free medium is one of the most important ways to prevent abnormal cells. There are also other factors which must be controlled to obtain high cell growth. The most important cell differentiation factors are the hormones and growth factors which are produced by the cell, which affect cell growth, differentiation, and proliferation. There are many other factors that also affect cell growth and differentiation, such as the cell attachment factors and the cell shape factors, the extracellular matrix, the extracellular matrix proteins, the extracellular matrix and matrix metalloproteinases, the soluble factors, and cell signaling molecules. This family of proteins includes extracellular signal-regulated kinases, c-Jun-NH2-terminal kinases, and p38 kinases. In addition, several cell attachment factors and cell attachment proteins have been identified which are involved in cell attachment and cell-cell interactions. This family of proteins includes the integrins, cadherins, and the integrin-associated proteins. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"{\\\"text\\\": \\\"Introduction\\\\n============\\\\n\\\\nThe incidence of hip osteoarthritis (HOA) has been reported to be between 15 and 17% of the United States population ([@b1-mmr-12-03-4445],[@b2-mmr-12-03-4445]). According to the International Classification of Diseases, 9th revision (ICD-9) codes, hip osteoarthritis accounts for 4.4% of patients with arthritis, in addition to other types of hip osteoarthritis, for example, lumbar spinal stenosis, spondylolysis, post-traumatic instability, osteochondritis dessicans, and gluteal tendinitis ([@b3-mmr-12-03-4445]). However, the pathogenesis of HOA remains unclear. As previous studies have reported, the risk factors include age, body weight, body height, physical activity and smoking ([@b4-mmr-12-03-4445],[@b5-mmr-12-03-4445]). Furthermore, patients who have undergone total hip arthroplasty (THA) can be associated with decreased activity levels due to their pain and functional limitations. The present study determined that the activity level of patients with HOA before surgery was different from those after surgery.\\\\n\\\\nIn particular, the majority of patients with HOA who undergo THA demonstrate a reduction in walking activity, and several previous studies have reported that patients\\\\\\\\' activity levels during postoperative follow-up should be measured, as it has been reported that walking can be a predictor of pain and other clinical outcomes in postoperative HOA patients ([@b6-mmr-12-03-4445]--[@b9-mmr-12-03-4445]). In addition, recent studies revealed the association between the decrease in walking activity and a long-term clinical prognosis in patients with hip OA ([@b10-mmr-12-03-4445],[@b11-mmr-12-03-4445]). Additionally, a study reported that the decrease in walking activity is a predictor of HOA ([@b12-mmr-12-03-4445]). Therefore, an accurate measurement of patients\\\\\\\\' walking activity after surgery is important, as it may indicate an improvement in HOA patients\\\\\\\\' functional condition, postoperative pain, quality of life, and long-term clinical prognosis.\\\\n\\\\nHowever, studies on the correlation between the preoperative walking activity of HOA patients and the postoperative walking activity are scarce. Some of them can result in acute or chronic toxicity. These include the effects of phosphines, dimethyl phosphines, and phosphocarbamate. The acute effects of the organophosphates include paralysis, ataxia, seizures, and coma. The chronic effects include necrosis of the central and peripheral nervous system, degeneration of the retina, and atrophy of the adrenal medulla. The chronic effects of the carbamates include hepatotoxicity, hemolytic anemia, and nervous system lesions. Among the effects of these pesticides, the effects of organophosphates on the brain are more dramatic than those of the carbamates. Some of the toxic effects of the pesticides may be due to their metabolic activation by acetylcholinesterase (AChE). The metabolic activation of these pesticides by AChE may cause a wide variety of toxic effects, such as paralysis, ataxia, coma, convulsions, and even death. In addition, these pesticides may act as neurotoxins. These neurotoxins include methyl- and dimethyl phosphates, phosphonylphosphonates, phosphonylphosphates, and phosphonylphosphonates. The metabolic activation of these pesticides by AChE may also cause severe neurotoxicity. These pesticides may be neurotoxicants, neurotoxins, or both. Some of the toxic effects of the pesticides may be caused by their direct toxic effects on the central nervous system. The direct toxic effects on the central nervous system include inhibition of acetylcholinesterase, inhibition of GABA-A and -B receptors, and depression of neurotransmitters. In addition, some of these pesticides can affect the function of ion channels, such as the sodium channels in the muscle cells and the potassium channels in the nerve cells. The direct toxic effects of some of these pesticides on nerve cells include inhibition of acetylcholinesterase and an increase in the levels of cyclic adenosine 3',5'-monophosphate. In addition, some of these pesticides may affect the neurotransmitters, and decrease the level of acetylcholine. If the code ratio stays flat or decreases as depth increases, it's probably fine—but if it amplifies with recursive training, that could be a signal issue worth investigating. % at depth-0, 0% at depth-1, ~5% at depth-2, maybe higher at depth-3), then the classifier might partially learn "more code = deeper depth" which is a confound. However:
1. depth-0 is Wikipedia (0% code by design)
2. depth-1 seems to have ~0% code (from my 8-sample check)
3. depth-2 has maybe ~5% code

This asymmetry could be a confound. But it's also a real signal of model collapse — the model amplifying certain modes in its training distribution. Whether this is a confound or a genuine depth signal depends on how you frame it.

For the MVP, this is probably not a blocking issue because:
- The code ratio is small (~5%)
- The classifier uses multiple features (perplexity stats, lexical diversity) that should pick up depth signal beyond just code

I can do a more rigorous analysis post-MVP to control for content type. I'll flag this as a warning to monitor at depth-3 and document it for the paper. But first, let me dig deeper into the depth-1 tail samples since I only spot-checked a few earlier — I want to use a more systematic approach to check for code content.
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me compile the code indicator counts:

| Pattern | depth-1 (5000 samples) | depth-2 (2095 samples) |
|---------|------------------------|------------------------|
| `import java` | 63 | 46 |
| `#include` | 132 | 101 |
| `Copyright (c)` | 72 | 110 |

Note: depth_2 currently has 2095 samples (still generating), so normalizing:


Now I'm calculating the normalized rates for each pattern across both depths. The results show some striking differences — `#include` statements jump from 2.64% at depth-1 to 4.82% at depth-2, an 82% increase, and copyright headers spike even more dramatically from 1.44% to 5.25%.

This reveals that code contamination is actually getting worse as I go deeper into the dataset. My earlier manual spot-checks of eight samples found nothing, but that was just bad luck — the contaminated samples are scattered throughout the file, and I happened to miss them in my small sample. my earlier "域一致性初步通过" assessment. depth-1 DOES have code content (~1-3% depending on indicator), and depth-2 has significantly more (~2-5%).

Actually, these counts might overcount because a single sample could contain multiple `#include` statements. Let me think about this differently. One JSON line = one sample. grep -c counts lines (samples) that match. So 132 depth-1 samples contain at least one `#include`, out of 5000 = 2.64%. That's reasonable.

But some samples might have `#include` in a natural language context (e.g., "you should #include the header file"). Let me not overthink this — the pattern is clear: there's a meaningful amount of code content in both depth-1 and depth-2, and it's increasing.

The code percentages show a clear trend: depth-0 has none by design, depth-1 sits around 2-3%, and depth-2 climbs to 3-5% with likely further increases at depth-3. This creates a potential confound where a classifier could pick up on code presence as a proxy for depth rather than learning true distributional shifts. But this actually reflects real model collapse — the model amplifies code from The Pile's ~3.3% during recursive training, which is a genuine depth signal even if it's content-type rather than statistical. For the MVP, this seems acceptable since the percentages remain small.

I should flag this as a warning with the specific data: my earlier assessment was premature, depth-1 isn't code-free, and the increasing code ratio could be either a confound or a legitimate depth manifestation. Post-MVP, I'll need to stratify by content type to disentangle these signals. The training loss also shows improvement from depth-0 (2.2411) to depth-1 (1.3894), which is encouraging.

The 38% loss drop suggests the model is fitting its own outputs more easily, consistent with distribution narrowing. I'm also checking whether the parallel feature extraction worker actually used GPU 1 as intended—the worker name and default settings suggest it did, though I can't verify the logs directly since it's already completed.

Looking at the GPU metrics during the pipeline run confirms this: GPU 0 was actively computing at 62-76% while GPU 1 sat idle, and the memory footprint aligns perfectly—GPU 0 shows 13759 MiB for just the Pythia generation while GPU 1 shows 0 MiB since feature extraction finished and freed its memory. This rules out any memory conflicts or GPU misallocation. Now I'm compiling these findings into a warning report.

_source line 686 · 2026-05-14T13:00:46.626Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
"def " is less reliable as an indicator because it can appear in natural English text too ("the definition of..."). But the numbers still support the trend:
- depth-1: 177/5000 = 3.54%
- depth-2: 98/2095 = 4.68%

Though "def " is ambiguous, so I'll focus on the more reliable indicators: `import java`, `#include`, and `Copyright (c)`.

Let me summarize the quantitative findings:

**Code indicator rates:**

| Indicator | depth-0 (5000) | depth-1 (5000) | depth-2 (2095, partial) |


|-----------|---------------|----------------|------------------------|
| `import java` | 0 (by design, Wikipedia) | 63 (1.26%) | 46 (2.20%) |
| `#include` | 0 | 132 (2.64%) | 101 (4.82%) |
| `Copyright (c)` | 0 | 72 (1.44%) | 110 (5.25%) |

Generating markdown table...

The code content ratio roughly doubles from depth-1 to depth-2 across all indicators, which signals mode amplification—a real phenomenon tied to model collapse. For the MVP, I need to flag this as both a genuine depth signal and a potential confound where the classifier might lean too heavily on content-type rather than actual statistical distribution shifts. The parallel worker's feature extraction also looks solid, so I should surface this finding as a warning.
```

</details>

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — mcp__agent-ml-research__reviewer_post_to_director · `toolu_01L79FWfhPc79F114anrwGxg`</summary>

```json
{
  "text": "## 审查报告更新 — mvp_pipeline_pythia_002 + cuda1-parallel-tasks\n\n**结论**: WARNING（修正之前的\"初步 PASS\"评估）\n\n### 发现\n\n#### 1. 代码污染定量分析 — 修正\"域一致性初步通过\"\n\n- [severity: warning] **depth-1 并非零代码，且 depth-2 代码比率显著上升**。我之前基于 8 个样本的手动抽查得出的\"域一致性通过\"结论不准确。定量检查结果：\n\n| 代码指标 | depth-0 (5000, Wikipedia) | depth-1 (5000) | depth-2 (2095, 生成中) |\n|---------|--------------------------|----------------|----------------------|\n| `import java` | 0 | 63 (1.26%) | 46 (2.20%) |\n| `#include` | 0 | 132 (2.64%) | 101 (4.82%) |\n| `Copyright (c)` | 0 | 72 (1.44%) | 110 (5.25%) |\n\n**代码内容比率从 depth-1 到 depth-2 约翻倍**。这是 The Pile 中 ~3.3% 代码内容在递归训练中的 mode amplification 效应。\n\n**对 MVP 的影响评估**：\n- 与 Qwen2.5 不同（17.9-100% 代码），Pythia 的代码比率仍低（<5%），feature-based 分类器不太可能仅靠\"有无代码\"就达到 >40% 准确率\n- 但 depth-0 = 0% 代码（Wikipedia）vs depth-1/2/3 = 递增代码 构成 **domain shift confound**：分类器可能部分学到的是\"更多代码=更深\"而非统计分布差异\n- 建议 MVP 后做 content-type stratified analysis：对仅自然语言子集重跑分类器，验证 depth signal 是否独立于 content-type shift\n- **不阻塞 MVP**：这是真实的 model collapse 现象（mode amplification），论文可正面讨论\n\n#### 2. Pipeline 进度\n\n- [severity: info] **depth-0 finetune**: 完成，loss 2.2411，268.3s\n- [severity: info] **depth-1 finetune**: 完成，loss 1.3894，267.7s。**loss 比 depth-0 下降 38%** — 模型对自身输出更容易拟合，符合 model collapse 理论预期（分布窄化）。这个 loss 趋势本身可能是有价值的 depth signal。\n- [severity: info] **depth-2 generation**: 进行中（2095/5000）\n- GPU 0: 76% compute，13.7GB/97.8GB — 正常\n\n#### 3. cuda1-parallel-tasks Worker 审查\n\n- [severity: info] **depth_0_features.jsonl** 已生成，5000 条记录，格式正确。包含完整特征集：mean/var/skewness/kurtosis_ppl、percentile-based PPL (p10-p90)、surprisal 统计、TTR、hapax_ratio、bigram/trigram entropy、rep_2/3/4gram。\n- [severity: info] extract_features.py 默认 `--gpu 1`，与 pipeline 的 GPU 0 不冲突。Worker 名称 `cuda1-parallel-tasks` 与代码默认值一致。GPU 边界合规。\n- [severity: info] 特征值合理：depth-0 sample 0 的 mean_ppl=1607, TTR=0.57, hapax_ratio=0.79, bigram_entropy=6.01 — 人类文本的典型特征范围。\n\n#### 4. 教训自查\n\n- [severity: info] **手动抽查 8 个样本不足以做域一致性判断**。depth-1 有 63-132 条（1.3-2.6%）含代码指标的样本，但我的 8 个样本全部自然语言。后续域一致性检查应改用 grep 定量扫描而非仅手动抽查。\n\n### 待完成\n- depth-2 完成后复查代码比率（当前基于 2095 条的估算）\n- depth-3 代码比率检查（预期继续上升）\n- depth-2/3 finetune loss 趋势跟踪\n- 特征提取全量完成后，检查 depth-wise 特征分布差异\n- MVP gate check 最终审查",
  "project": "recursive_gen_depth_est"
}

For depth_1: rough code contamination estimate:

For depth_2, the partial data from 2095 samples shows higher rates: around 4.8% with "#include", 5.2% with copyright headers, and 2.2% with Java imports, suggesting contamination might be climbing at greater depths.

The pattern is striking—code contamination increases from depth-1 to depth-2, which aligns with what I'd expect from mode amplification during model collapse. It's a useful signal but also something I need to account for when evaluating the classifier.

I'm noticing a clear degradation pattern across depths within each seed_id — the content becomes progressively shallower and less coherent as depth increases, whether it's natural language or code.

The key insight is that since generation starts from the BOS token with a fixed seed (42) and batch_size=16, each seed_id occupies the same position in the random number generator sequence across all depths. So seed_id 1 at depth-1 and seed_id 1 at depth-2 are drawing from the same RNG state, just with different model weights. This explains why the content is thematically related but varies in quality and domain depending on which model generated it.

The striking pattern where seed_id=1 produces Java code at multiple depths suggests the code-generating mode is a stable attractor in the model's output distribution — once it enters that mode, it persists even as the underlying weights change. This is interesting for understanding the research question, though it could introduce confounds in the classifier training.

Now I need to retrieve the training losses, but the grep approach hit a limitation, so I'm trying a different method.
```

Excellent! Now I have all the data I need.

**Training Loss Trend** (monotonically decreasing — strong model collapse signal):
- depth-0 finetune: 2.2411 (268.3s)
- depth-1 finetune: 1.3894 (267.7s) — 38% decrease
- depth-2 finetune: 1.0250 (267.7s) — 26% decrease from depth-1

This is a clear monotonic decrease: 2.24 → 1.39 → 1.03. 

The model is progressively better at predicting its own output, which is exactly the model collapse signature. This supports the hypothesis that recursive generation creates distinguishable patterns.

**Code Contamination across Depths** (using "#include" as proxy, which is the most sensitive indicator):
- depth-0: 0 (Wikipedia, by design)
- depth-1: 132/5000 = 2.6%
- depth-2: 101/2095 — but I need to recheck this since the depth-2 dataset was still incomplete when I sampled it.

Let me look at the full contamination picture across multiple proxies. Using "Copyright (c)" as a less noisy indicator, I'm seeing 1.4% at depth-1 and 6.9% at depth-3. The "#include" proxy shows 2.6% at depth-1 and 4.7% at depth-3. I also started checking "import java" but the data's incomplete there too.

What's striking is the consistent pattern: code contamination is climbing with depth. Across all three proxies, I'm seeing 3-5x increases from shallow to deep layers. This looks like a real mode amplification effect, not just noise. The key question now is whether the classifier's performance gains are actually coming from detecting code versus natural language, or if this is genuinely part of what makes deeper layers more useful.

Let me pull the complete depth-2 dataset to verify those partial numbers and get a clearer picture of the full trend.

_source line 749 · 2026-05-14T13:51:24.110Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the complete picture for code contamination:

**"#include" counts** (proxy for C/C++ code):
- depth-0: 1 (Wikipedia might mention #include in context)
- depth-1: 132/5000 = 2.6%
- depth-2: 206/5000 = 4.1%
- depth-3: 234/5000 = 4.7%

**"Copyright (c)" counts** (proxy for code headers):
- depth-0: 0


- depth-1: 72/5000 = 1.4%
- depth-2: 216/5000 = 4.3%
- depth-3: 347/5000 = 6.9%

**"import java" counts** (proxy for Java code):
- depth-0: 0
- depth-1: 63/5000 = 1.3%
- depth-2: 110/5000 = 2.2%
- depth-3: 189/5000 = 3.8%

All three proxies show a consistent monotonic increase with depth, which points to mode amplification—a classic model collapse pattern where overrepresented patterns get reinforced as the model trains on its own outputs.

These are line-level grep matches, so there's overlap between patterns (a Java file would trigger both "import java" and "Copyright" checks), meaning the actual contamination is lower than summing the percentages, but the upward trend is unmistakable. Now I'm putting together the full review with the key findings: the pipeline processed 20,000 samples across four depths, training loss decreased monotonically confirming model collapse, and code contamination climbed from roughly 1-3% at depth-1 to 4-7% at depth-3.

The code contamination is the critical finding here—it's a warning-level issue because it could bias the classifier toward learning code patterns rather than depth signals, though it's also a legitimate indicator of mode amplification during recursive generation. The feature extraction should pick up both code-specific and general patterns, and this needs to be flagged in the MVP documentation. The monotonic loss decrease is actually encouraging though, since it suggests the model's perplexity on its own outputs is dropping, which the external reference model's perplexity features should capture. I'm also noticing that depth-3 outputs are noticeably shorter and simpler than depth-1, and there are some interesting variations across different seed samples.

The key constraint I'm working with is that only the depth-0 features have been extracted so far using GPU 1, while both GPUs are now idle. The pipeline generates the data but doesn't handle feature extraction—that's a separate downstream step I still need to run for depths 1 through 3.
```

</details>

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — mvp_pipeline_pythia_002（完成审查）\n\n**结论**: WARNING — pipeline 执行成功且数据质量基本合格，但发现代码内容随深度递增的域漂移，需在分类器评估时控制\n\n### 数据完整性 ✅\n- depth_0.jsonl: 5000 条（11.0MB，Wikipedia 种子数据）\n- depth_1.jsonl: 5000 条（9.2MB）\n- depth_2.jsonl: 5000 条（9.3MB）\n- depth_3.jsonl: 5000 条（9.7MB）\n- depth_0_features.jsonl: 5000 条（3.4MB，parallel worker 提前在 GPU 1 提取）\n- 合计 20,000 生成样本 + 5,000 种子样本\n\n### 训练 Loss 趋势 ✅ （强信号）\n| 步骤 | Final Loss | Runtime |\n|------|-----------|---------|\n| depth-0 LoRA | 2.2411 | 268.3s |\n| depth-1 LoRA | 1.3894 | 267.7s |\n| depth-2 LoRA | 1.0250 | 267.7s |\n\nLoss 单调递减（2.24 → 1.39 → 1.03），说明模型对自身生成的文本越来越容易拟合——这是 model collapse 的直接证据，也强烈支持核心假设：不同深度的文本具有可区分的统计模式。\n\n### [severity: warning] 代码内容随深度递增（域漂移 / 模式放大）\n\n用三个代码标志 grep 全量数据：\n\n| 指标 | depth-0 | depth-1 | depth-2 | depth-3 |\n|------|---------|---------|---------|---------|\n| \"#include\" 匹配行 | 1 | 132 (2.6%) | 206 (4.1%) | 234 (4.7%) |\n| \"Copyright (c)\" | 0 | 72 (1.4%) | 216 (4.3%) | 347 (6.9%) |\n| \"import java\" | 0 | 63 (1.3%) | 110 (2.2%) | 189 (3.8%) |\n\n（注：同一样本可能触发多个指标，实际代码样本比例低于指标之和；百分比是行级匹配 / 5000）\n\n趋势清晰：**代码内容随深度单调递增**。这是 The Pile（~3.3% 代码）中代码 mode 被递归训练放大的典型 model collapse 现象。\n\n**风险**：分类器可能学到\"代码比例高 = 深度大\"而非真正的深度信号（lexical/perplexity 退化）。由于 depth-0 是纯 Wikipedia（0% 代码），depth-1 ~2%，depth-3 ~5-7%，这构成潜在的域混淆。\n\n**建议**：\n1. MVP 评估时增加 **content-type 控制实验**：仅在自然语言样本上评估分类器（过滤含代码标志的样本），对比全量结果。若过滤后 accuracy 大幅下降，说明分类器依赖域信号而非深度信号。\n2. 在论文中将\"代码模式放大\"作为 model collapse 的证据之一来讨论，而非忽略。\n3. 这不阻塞 MVP——3-7% 的代码混入远优于 Qwen2.5 的 17.9-100%，且特征提取包含 perplexity/lexical 多维度指标。\n\n### [severity: info] 域一致性抽查（通过）\n- depth-1 head/middle/tail 共 11 条样本：全部自然语言（论坛帖、学术论文、商业描述、Q&A、博客）\n- depth-2 head 3 条：2 自然语言 + 1 Java 代码\n- depth-3 head 3 条：2 自然语言 + 1 Java 代码（与 depth-2 同一 seed_id=1）\n- 定性观察：同一 seed_id 的文本随深度变得更简短、更泛化、更重复——model collapse 的定性表现\n\n### [severity: info] GPU 边界合规\nPipeline 全程使用 cuda:0，parallel worker 使用 cuda:1。均在项目分配范围内。pipeline 结束后两张 GPU 均空闲（0%，0 MiB）。\n\n### [severity: info] seed data tokenizer 不一致（非阻塞）\nprepare_seed_data.py 用 Qwen2.5-1.5B tokenizer 筛选 256-512 tokens，实际训练用 Pythia tokenizer。对内容无影响，论文写作时注明即可。\n\n### [severity: info] Registry 状态\nmvp_pipeline_pythia_002 已转为 ANALYZING。spec 完整（含 hypothesis、hyperparams、seed、model 路径等）。\n\n### 待办\n- 等待 depth_1/2/3 特征提取完成\n- 审查分类器训练结果\n- MVP gate check（4-class acc >40%, adjacent AUC >0.6）\n- 建议增加 content-type 控制对比",

The features_all.jsonl file has only 1162 lines and they're all depth-0, which is a problem since the classifier needs features from multiple depths. I need to figure out whether the analysis worker is still running, hit an error, or if there's analysis output I haven't found yet — maybe in a separate log, notebook, or classifier results file.

- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

示例格式:
&lt;handoff>
### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目只能用 cuda:4 / cuda:5（之前两次违规 cuda:0 被 Director CRITICAL）
- submit_training_job 不支持 shell builtin（source/conda activate 全不行）

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 服务器：westd-8x6000，工作目录 /root/autodl-tmp/hybrid_reasoning_phases
- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

### 3. 📊 当前状态
我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
registry: pilot_011 RUNNING；其他全 DONE。

### 4. 📋 待办
- pilot_011 cron 回调 → spawn worker 跑 probe + GC4 计算
- GC4 ≥2pp → 转架构 narrative；<2pp → null-result paper outline 已就绪

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
Falcon-H1R 是最后一个数据点。已知 4B/9B GC4 都 FAIL。
易错：不要把 GC4-par (Falcon parallel) 和 GC4-seq (Qwen sequential) 直接对比。
&lt;/handoff>
